Astonished and amazed, the simple doctor could not help wondering why this aristocratic and eminent personality, with all the resources at his command, should have come all the way from Bihar to consult with him!

Voicing his surprise the doctor inquired, "Who has sent you to me?" Shashi Babu gave Baba's reference and mentioned both His worldly name of Shri Prabhat Rainjan Sarkar and His spiritual name of Shrii Shrii Anandamurtiji. The doctor was all the more surprised, for he had never heard of Baba, neither by His worldly name nor as a spiritual Master.

The doctor was frank enough to confess, "I have no special knowledge or experience about this type of disease. However, since you have full faith in your Guru, I have no objections in seeing the patient." Shashi Babu fixed the date of the appointment and brought Guddu from Muzaffarpur on the appointed day.

The doctor examined Guddu, and after thorough investigation he was still unable to diagnose the nature of the illness. However, with his limited knowledge and experience, he proceeded to treat the boy with some medicines and massages. This treatment continued for about twenty to twenty-five days. As the days passed, the doctor began to feel great concern and sympathy for his patient. He thought to himself, "How unfortunate it is that such a young boy, coming from such a good and noble family, should have to undergo so much suffering. How helpless he is! Yet he is undergoing my treatment with such good faith and hope and sincerity. God willing, may he get well soon."

“Práner Spandana Yuga Vivartana”

Even as the doctor's mind became flooded with these kind and compassionate thoughts, so also there came about a change in Guddu's condition. Gradually he began to feel stronger, healthier. From a bedridden patient, Guddu slowly and steadily was able to walk with a stick. The doctor felt reassured by his patient's improvement and was hopeful and confident of his recovery.

Shashi Babu's life was full of commitments and responsibilities and hence he was unable to stay on in Burdwan for an indefinite period. He requested the doctor to write down the prescription and the line of treatment and assured him that they would follow his advice meticulously. The doctor had no objections, and so Shashi Babu and Guddu returned to Muzaffarpur. The Lord knows full well that Guddu faithfully continued with the treatment. But strangely, after his return to Muzaffarpur there was a sudden halt in his progress. The improvement that had set in, while under the personal supervision of the doctor, did not continue once he returned to Muzaffarpur. However, he was now at least fortunate to be able to move around with the help of a stick.

It was the month of July 1971 and Baba had been admitted to the Patna Medical College for treatment. The specialists had prescribed several tests and investigations, and Baba had to be taken in a wheel-chair from one department to another for these tests. One day even as Baba was being wheeled out of His room, Shashi Babu and his son Kartik were waiting outside, hands folded in expectation of His darshana. Baba immediately greeted them with a namaskar and simultaneously blessed them. A Margii brother happened to be pushing Baba's chair from behind. Guddu managed to get behind Baba and silently entreated the attending disciple to allow him to push Baba's chair. The attendant seeing Guddu's physical condition hesitated for a moment. Shashi Babu thereupon gestured to him with a humble request to allow Guddu to push Baba's chair and the brother relented. Baba seemingly remained unaware of the silent gesturing and pleading that resulted in the change of attendants. Guddu, limping and with a great effort, slowly managed to wheel Baba to the room of the particular medical specialist.

“Práner Spandana Yuga Vivartana”

Upon reaching there Baba asked, "Tell me! Who has been pushing my wheel chair today, and has brought me here?" Baba had barely spoken these words when Guddu rushed forward and fell at His Feet. Baba appeared surprised and said,

"Oh is that you, Guddu! Were you pushing my chair? But you yourself are unwell so how could you push my chair? Come here, come here. Let me see what is wrong with you?" Saying this Baba, while remaining seated in His chair, gestured to Guddu to stand up. As Guddu drew closer, Baba passed His hand over Guddu's entire body. With His touch it seemed as though Baba had waved a magic wand! Guddu felt as though an electric current had touched him, and with a sudden

jolt something seemed to have gone out of his body. Immediately a surge of energy engulfed his body and slowly he regained his normalcy.

Oh Supreme Lord, merciful and compassionate one, how amazing is Your liila! Here You are, seated in a patient's chair, seemingly awaiting treatment for Yourself. Like a helpless patient You are allowing Yourself to be wheeled around from one medical department to another, and yet, just by Your very touch You are able to banish another's hitherto incurable sickness! Wondrous indeed are Your deeds! Having "stolen" the samskaras of others and having taken over the burden of their sins, You silently bear their consequences! Oh my Beloved Master! Kind and compassionate, caring and forgiving, there is no end to Your grace and mercy!

“Práner Spandana Yuga Vivartana”

Those who were fortunate enough to bear witness to this miraculous transformation, were blessed indeed. As Hamrahi's pen relates this great wonder, his eyes are streaming with tears. It is indeed hard to imagine the mental and emotional state of those devotees who actually witnessed this great liila.

The news of this miracle created a great stir amongst the devotees who became ecstatic with emotion. Overwhelmed with an upsurge of devotion, they remained inarticulate and speechless. Sujitji is a householder Acarya who also hails from Muzaffarpur. He was closely associated with Shashi Babu's family and was well aware of every aspect of Guddu's illness, from its onset to its very end. Ever since he had heard about Guddu's wondrous recovery, he had been astounded and amazed. A question arose in his mind, and like the smoke emanating from a half-lit damp wood, it spread till it engulfed and choked him. He was well aware that if Baba so desired, He could have cured Guddu by His samkalpa alone, by His thought waves alone. Even a compassionate glance of His would have restored Guddu to health. That He gave Guddu a chance to serve Him in the hospital and took the opportunity of relieving him of his samskaras was also understandable. But Sujitji could not comprehend as to why Baba sent Guddu to faraway Burdwan, to be treated by an unknown, small town doctor. Why did Guddu have to undergo all the pain and suffering involved in this long-drawn treatment? It was a 'secret' that Sujitji was more than curious to know. In fact this burning question, this overwhelming curiosity was causing him much mental turmoil. He could barely wait to get an opportunity to meet with Baba and get the answer from Him!

“Práner Spandana Yuga Vivartana”

At last Sujitji got an opportunity to accompany Baba on His field walk. The long-suppressed question surfaced in no time and he put it across to Baba. Baba smiled and good-humouredly tried to evade the question. He changed the topic, but Sujitji could not be put off so easily. Like a stubborn child he continued to question Baba. While returning from the field walk, Baba turned to Sujitji and said, "I am about to relate to you a true story, that took place about nine hundred years ago. In a village in the Jalpaiguri district of North Bengal, there lived a zamindar (a land-holder), a good and kindly man. He was prosperous and wealthy and well-provided for in every way. In spite of his life of ease and comfort, he used to remain greatly disturbed and had lost his peace of mind. The cause of his distress was his eldest son, who was a depraved, ill-tempered, insolent and rascally boy. He was irresponsible and indulgent, wayward in his ways, and recklessly spent his father's wealth. The zamindar tried very hard to persuade his son to mend his ways, but the boy did not change, and the father remained distraught and disappointed. Finally, out of sheer exasperation, the father was forced to put a restriction on his son's spending habits. In fact he disinherited his eldest son.

"When the son got to know of this, he was filled with rage. Seething with anger, he forced himself into his father's bedroom. It was afternoon and the zamindar was resting awhile. The son woke him out of his sleep and began to rant and rave. His speech was confused and incoherent and utterly senseless. As it is, the father in his distress had lost all his sleep and now he was annoyed by his son's uncontrollable behaviour. In fact he was half-afraid too, for he did not like the look in the boy's eyes.

“Práner Spandana Yuga Vivartana”

"First, like the understanding father that he was, he tried to reason with his son. He told the son clearly that his decision to disinherit him was not irrevocable. The father told his son that he was prepared to change his mind provided the son would give up his vices and change his contentious behaviour. For a long time this argument continued, and loud and angry voices could be heard from within the room. But it was impossible to persuade or convince this rascally boy. The combination of youth, an innately foul temper, and the influence of alcohol proved dangerous indeed. The boy lost all self-control. He spotted a stick lying nearby, picked it up and began to beat his father mercilessly. Helpless to defend himself the father continued to take the

beating. A deadly blow on the head left him bleeding profusely and in no time the father succumbed to his injury."

After relating all this, Baba suddenly became silent. Sujitji was utterly confused. He failed to see any connection between Baba's story and his earlier question, and wondered why Baba had brought this up.

Baba sensed the Acarya's confusion and by way of clarification said, "Do you know Sujit! Dr. Shailendra Mukherjee in his previous life time was the very same village zamindar and Kartik himself was his ill-tempered son. Nine hundred years later as the reactive samskaras of his past evil deeds ripened in this life-time, Guddu's hands and limbs gradually became paralyzed. As per the accumulated samskaras of his previous lives, Prakrti spread her net and created the complicated circumstances in which Guddu was cruelly ensnared. The reason I sent Guddu to Dr. Shailendra Mukherjee (who was his father, many life-times back), was that if the doctor was able to develop love and compassion for Guddu's condition, then there would be a possibility of Guddu's samskaras being exhausted. During the days that Guddu spent with Dr. Mukherjee, undergoing his treatment, the doctor developed feelings of great sympathy and compassion for his patient. Guddu was cured not by the doctor's medicine but by his kindness and affection. Thus whatever was possible was done and the remaining samskaras were removed by me. This then is the 'secret' behind this incident."

“Práner Spandana Yuga Vivartana”

By now, Sujitji's mind was in a whirl, drowned as he was like an amateur diver, in Baba's mysterious whirlpool. Baba turned to him and asked, "What's the matter Sujit? Where are you?"

Sujitji was stunned and stood motionless, staring at Baba speechless and lost in thought. His mind began to wonder: "Oh Lord! Infinite and endless are Your liilas! In this birth, countless creatures take birth and die, and this constant cycle of birth and rebirth has neither a beginning nor an end. How does one recall and explain an incident that took place nine hundred years back in the life of a village zamindar? Stranger still, how does one relate this to another incident that takes place nine hundred years later, as in the case of Shailendra Mukherjee and his meeting with Kartik? How amazing indeed is the role of samskaras, the ripening of samskaras accumulated over centuries, the painful experience of their reactions; and then to learn about their final exhaustion only through Your compassionate grace! With the passage of time, enormous evolutionary changes take place through the ages, and countless living beings are caught in this cycle of birth and death. Yet You are aware of each one of them, You are aware every moment, of their every action. In fact every act and incident seems to be meticulously recorded in Your divine diary! How mysterious You are my Lord! Unfathomable and boundless You are! You are all-pervasive; indescribable! Words are inadequate to express Your majesty! You are bound by none, You indeed are the Supreme Desideratum. You are beyond the grasp of human feelings, beyond all mental conception, You are beyond the scope of time; eternal You are my Lord!"

Sujitji's thoughts raced on. "Lord, Your gaze is everywhere and in all directions. You are omniscient, all-pervading, You are indeed all in all. You are glorious, effulgent and self-existent. You are the Supreme Brahma. You are easily appeased, O You who are auspicious to behold! O Supreme Guide! O compassionate, all-merciful and tender-hearted Baba! O wonderful and magnanimous Lord! You are the personification of heavenly delight, the very garden of paradise, oh exquisite Lord, You are the essence of beauty and grace!"

“Práner Spandana Yuga Vivartana”

Engulfed in these ecstatic thoughts of Baba's divinity and mystery, Sujitji remained lost in Baba's magical world of liilas, his mind echoing and re-echoing with the praises of his Guru.

Mahimnah páram te paramavidusho yady asadrshii,
Stutir brahmádiinám api tad avasannás tvayi girah.
Athávácyah sarvah svam atiparinámávadhi grnan,
Mamápy esha stotre hara nirapavádah parikarah.
("If the extent of Your praise even by one supremely learned is unbecoming, then so also are the praise hymns of the gods Brahma and so forth; speaking of You would come to an end. And if no one is to be censured by praising You to the extent of their limitations, then my undertaking this hymn, O Hara, is also blameless.")

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

In the realm of sadhana, the importance of kiirtana cannot be overemphasized. Through kiirtana the body and the mind get invigorated and refreshed. The mind gets vibrated and concentration on one's ishta cakra is quick and spontaneous. The practice of the three shuddhis facilitates this initial process, but with kiirtana this is very easily achieved. Hence for every sadhaka it is important and beneficial to perform kiirtana before starting one's sadhana practice.

Collective kiirtana, when performed continuously over long stretches of twenty-four hours, vibrates the environment. The waves of devotion and spiritual rapture thus created are unique and indescribable. Through kiirtana the mind is quickly transported into a state of spiritual ecstacy; hence it is a vital aid for a spiritual aspirant. Referring to kiirtana, Chintaharan Sharma has said:

The appearance of kiirtana in the realm of spiritual sadhana was due to its special quality which helps the limited unit mind to transcend itself and merge with the Cosmic Mind. Prakrti too responds with delight when engulfed with its blissful vibrations. Like the wafted fragrance of pollen from a flowering meadow, kiirtana's sweet vibrations fill the air with tidings of joy and gladness.

Kiirtana enabled the well-known poet-saint of Birbhum, Candidas, to reach his spiritual goal. Mahaprabhu Caitanya too became one with his beloved Lord Krishna through the powerful medium of kiirtana. Mahaprabhu introduced the devotional wave of kiirtana and proclaimed "kiirtanaiiyah sada Harih" ("one should always sing the kiirtana of the Lord"). The Lord is ever present wheresoever His devotees sing His kiirtana.

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

Bengal's devotional poet Narottamdas Thakur says:

"Krishnn nám harinám badai madhur,
Je jan Krishna bhaje se bada catur"

("The name of Krishna, the name of Hari, is extremely sweet, and one devoted to Krishna is extremely clever"). Over and over again, on many occasions Baba expounded and extolled the importance of kiirtana, and re-established it as an integral part of sadhana.

He often used to say:

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

"Náham tishthámi vaikunthe, yoginám hridaye na cha;
madbhaktá yatra gáyante, tatra tishtha'mi Nárada"

("I do not dwell in heaven, nor in the hearts of the Yogiis; where my devotees sing, there do I reside, O Nárada").

The Lord does not prefer to stay in Heaven nor in the fabled Ocean of Milk. Neither does He choose to reside in the heart of Hathayogis or with those who dwell in the forests. His favourite abode is where His beloved devotees gather together and are absorbed in singing His Name.

All the inferiority-complexes of the mind are washed away in the devotional flow of kiirtana. It enables one to transcend all thoughts of discord and discrimination and liberates the mind from all complexes. The combination of rhythm and dance in kiirtana lends it unique charm and appeal. Indeed, lalita ("graceful") dance is the most appropriate style for the performance of kiirtana.

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

Continuously conducting seminars helped spread the ideology of the Marga amongst the sadhakas. As a result of this well-organized pracara, faith and conviction were strengthened and revived. It was at this juncture, when the seeds of jnana and bhakti had sprouted, that Baba introduced kiirtana, and incorporated it with the regular sadhana practices.

For the benefit of historians, it may be mentioned that the "Baba Náma Kevalam" eight-syllabic kiirtana was first introduced by Baba on 8th October 1970. At that time, Baba was staying at the Aamjharia Forest Guest House, situated about sixty kilometers from Ranchi. He had gone there to rest for five days. As a matter of fact in the context of Baba's life, the mention of the word "rest" would be absurd indeed. Mentally He remained constantly pre-occupied and every moment was utilized in planning and organizing innumerable programs for the benefit of human society. So while apparently resting within the natural and sanctified forest environment, the moment seemed ripe and auspicious for the introduction of kiirtana, one of Baba's unique gifts to mankind. Gurudeva for the first time initiated the "Baba Náma Kevalam" kiirtana, which would transport the devotee into the spiritually uplifting "madhurya bhava."

It is said that Acarya Brahmadevaji was the first to hear this holy chant, even as Baba invited him to listen to its resonance in the cosmos. Baba called Brahmadevaji and made him sit beside Him, saying:

Baba: Are you able to hear something?

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

Brahmadevaji: No Baba.

Baba: Make a little effort...now can you hear something?

Brahmadevaji: Yes Baba! I'm able to hear something, but it's very unclear.

Baba: Concentrate and try to listen more carefully.

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

Brahmadevaji: Yes Baba! It seems like the sound of some kiirtana.

Baba: What are the words of the kiirtana?

Brahmadevaji: The words are very indistinct.

Baba: Try a little harder.

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

Brahmadevaji: Yes Baba! The words are "Baba Náma Kevalam".

For quite a long while the sound of this holy mantra continued to be heard loud and clear, and its vibrations reverberated in the cosmos. Thereupon Baba directed all those who were present there to do kiirtana. So for the first time kiirtana to the chant of "Baba Náma Kevalam" was performed and the sadhakas were transported into a state of ecstacy and delight.

Baba went on to say that this kiirtana had the power of inducing consciousness into a hitherto inanimate object. Soon after, in the same year, on the auspicious occasion of Deepavali, another radiant lamp was lit, the flame of which would dispel the deepest darkness. That day in Ranchi, Baba performed the spiritual rite of empowering the "Baba Náma Kevalam" mantra, and thereupon proclaimed it as a mahamantra and a siddhamantra. Thereafter, Baba the Lord of Dance, introduced the appropriate mudras into the lalita dance form and made it the medium for the performance of kiirtana. He personally taught this dance to the sadhakas, and made it an integral and mandatory part of all future dharmic functions. It was announced that every Dharma Mahacakra would be preceded by a twenty-four hour akhanda kiirtana, and a seventy-two hour akhanda kiirtana was made compulsory prior to the annual Dharma Mahacakra.

Baba the destroyer of evil, the hope of the sinners, declared:

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

"Baba Náma Kevalam jato pápa hare,
pápider sa'dhya nai tato papa kare:"

the "Baba Náma Kevalam" eight-syllabic kiirtana can destroy more sins than even the worst sinners can possibly commit. Such then is the unprecedented greatness of this kiirtana.

For the very first time, a seventy-two hour akhanda kiirtana was performed in Patna, on the occasion of the Dharma Mahacakra held on the first of January 1971. It was a devotional and inspiring experience, as the large gathering of sadhakas danced and sang to the sweet melodies and beat of the kiirtana. After this, an extraordinary surge of devotion was felt and kiirtana began to be sung and performed all around the world. Gradually the sadhakas began to realize the amazing and incredibly subtle benefits of kiirtana. It proved to be the most effective antidote in times of trouble and misfortune. Kiirtana's powerful vibration diffuses and destroys all negative and evil forces and enables the devotee to face the most frightening situations. Ultimately evil itself is forced to admit defeat at the feet of the victorious devotee.

One has heard many stories and incidents in which the beneficial effects of kiirtana have been explicitly realized and experienced. However, one incident barely heard about comes to mind, which is related here. This reveals the subtle and mysterious way in which kiirtana helps stall an impending evil.

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

Pradyumnaji is one of Baba's most devoted disciples. He hails from the Purnea district of Bihar, and belongs to the zamindar community. His family is well known in his village of Roopaspur, which falls within the Mirganj district of Purnea. His father Sudhanshuji was the leader of the Bihar Legislative Assembly. Pradyumnaji was known for his generosity and munificence. His wife belonged to a princely family from the Palamu district of Bihar. At the time of his marriage, Pradyumnaji had not been initiated. It was said that he had received great wealth by way of a dowry, and this included a precious necklace estimated to be worth nine lakhs of rupees. News of this priceless gift had spread very quickly, and it was talked about in all the many villages around Roopaspur. If walls are said to have four ears then thieves indeed have eight!

A notorious gang of dacoits was known to roam the area in and around Roopaspur village, and sure enough, they too came to hear about the by-now famous necklace. The leader of this gang, one Banvari by name, operated from his base in the Benipatti village. The necklace of nine lakhs proved a tempting bait; after all, he thought, nothing much was served by small and petty thefts. If he could lay his hands on this necklace then the future would be secure indeed! So he decided to organize and lead a raid on Pradyumnaji's house. Having made up his mind, he was eagerly awaiting an opportune moment. Already he had deputed one of his agents to keep a watch on the goings-on around

Pradyumnaji's house, and Banvari was regularly receiving reports from his man.

Meanwhile it was announced that Dharma Mahacakra would be held in Patna on 1st January 1971. Pradyumnaji decided to attend the DMC, and planned to take his entire family along. So with the exception of his aged mother, the rest of the family left for Patna. The agent immediately sent word to Banvari and there was great excitement in his camp. After all, when would such an opportunity come again! Banvari formed a group of three thieves which included two other experienced dacoits, besides himself. Their names were Hari and Bateshwar. The three of them met and conspired to raid Pradyumnaji's house on the night of 31st December. They planned to leave separately from three different areas and decided to meet up at a spot close to the house.

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

On coming together, one of the dacoits decided to walk up to the main door, prior to breaking into the house. However, the dacoit was in for a rude shock. There in front of the main gate was an armed guard astride a tall mount; he was keeping watch over the house! The three of them waited for a long while, shivering as they did in the bitterly cold December night. Finally fear and apprehension got the better of them and they gave up their plan to raid the house that night. In fact they postponed it to the next night.

This time they decided to meet at the rear of the house. Banvari made a thorough survey of the bungalow. To the right of the house he spotted a tall tree right next to the boundary wall. Banvari climbed up this tree and planned to enter into the house. But as he did so, he was astonished to hear the loud sound of bhajana and kiirtana emanating from the house. He thought to himself that perhaps the old mother must have arranged for a Satyanarayan Puja to be performed on the occasion of the New Year. Hence kiirtana and readings from the scriptures were being heard. However, Banvari was sure that the guests would not stay on much longer; they would surely go home once the puja was over.

With this in mind, the three dacoits hid themselves in a nearby field. It was lush with freshly grown peas. When they returned after a while, the singing was still going on! Now they began to worry, as nothing seemed to be going according to their plans. They were so sure of their success, had come so close to achieving their ends, and suddenly this opportunity was slipping by. They became restless and wondered as to how the kiirtana seemed to be continuing endlessly; it was most unusual. Anger and frustration at being foiled in their evil designs coupled with the biting cold of the Paush night left them trembling and shivering. The chill seemed to have pierced their very bones! In spite of this, their greed was unabated and the thought of laying their hands on that "nine-lakh" necklace was enticing indeed. So they decided to give it one more try.

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

Once more they returned to the field and waited. It was now three hours before dawn, and the gang made tracks towards the house. But to their dismay they could hear that same kiirtana and singing from within the house. This time they lost all hopes, for their arch-enemy dawn was threatening to make its presence felt any moment now, and they worried about their safety. Dejected, they finally abandoned their plan of raiding Pradyumnaji's house. But how could they return empty-handed? After all, they had come with a mission. No one has ever heard of a wedding party returning home without the bride! So they decided to raid the neighbouring house. Close by, about three hundred yards away from Pradyumnaji's bungalow, was the house of his paternal cousin Haribabu. The dacoits broke into that house, raided it, and were content with whatever they could lay their hands on.

The next day, as the burglary came to light, there was an uproar in the entire village. On his return from the Dharma Mahacakra, Pradyumnaji too got the news of the theft. He was quite amazed and could not help wondering why the thieves did not attack his quiet and deserted house and instead raided the neighbouring bungalow. Then his thoughts reverted to his aged mother and the elderly woman who looked after her, and he shuddered to think as to how the two helpless women would have possibly faced the thieves had they broken into his house. Mentally he thanked Baba for His boundless Grace.

Days passed and it so happened that Pradyumnaji had to attend to some work in Ranchi. While there he also went for darshana to Baba's quarters. As he entered the quarters he met with Vishnudevji, Baba's body-guard. Right away Vishnudevji asked him, "Was there a burglary in your house recently?"

Pradyumnaji replied: "No, not in my house, the theft took place in my cousin Haribabu's house." Thereupon Vishnudevji went on to give a detailed account of the incident even as he had heard it related by Baba Himself during one of His evening walks. Vishnudevji told Pradyumnaji how that evening Baba had brought up the topic of kiirtana and was extolling its merits. Baba told the accompanying Margiis that even the gravest adversity can be easily averted with the help of kiirtana.

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

To further illustrate His point, Baba spoke about Pradyumnaji, and mentioned all that had transpired with his family. Baba continued to relate that at the very moment when Pradyumnaji was participating in the Dharma Mahacakra kiirtana, a gang of three robbers had gone to raid his bungalow in Roopaspur village. At that time there was only the aged mother and her maid-servant in the house.

Baba went on to say, "How was it possible for these poor frail ladies to confront those three sturdy men! Under those circumstances, I was compelled to do something. I immediately transferred the sound waves of that collective akhanda kiirtana to Pradyumnaji's house. On hearing the loud resonance of the kiirtana the thieves were alarmed. They waited for a long while and finally abandoned their plans."

Pradyumnaji could hardly believe what Vishnudevaji had just related! He thought to himself that Vishnudevaji must have made-up this story just to arouse faith in the benefits of kiirtana. No doubt a lot of stir had been created by the dacoity that had taken place in his village. Vishnudevaji too must have heard about it, and building on this, had come up with a highly imaginative story! Pradyumnaji cast it off as a figment of Vishnudevaji's imagination.

Some days after the incident, Banvari was caught when he tried to sell the revolver stolen from Haribabu's house. Soon after Hari and Bateshwar too fell into the net. They were beaten up in the police lock-up, and when interrogated, the three confessed and related the entire episode. They spoke about their original intention to steal the necklace, "the nine-lakhs necklace," and related all they had heard and experienced in the course of those two nights. When Pradyumnaji and Haribabu went to the police station, the dacoits narrated that same story over again.

"Kiirtanaiiyah Sadá Harih

Pradyumnaji was too stunned to react, for he recalled all that Vishnudevaji had told him. He was overcome with emotion and began to sob uncontrollably. He had realized and understood the immense grace showered upon him by the ever-compassionate Lord. Eternal is the Lord and infinite is His grace!

A note from the investigating authority: One of the three robbers is still alive today. Banvari was killed in an encounter with the police. Hari died of an incurable disease. After careful inquiry and a thorough search, the third and only surviving thief, Bateshwar, was contacted. On meeting him in person it was possible to get from him the full details of the above episode. This, then, was his story.

---

trńád api suniicena taror api sahishnuná,
amáninám mánadena kiirtaniiyah sadá Harih.
("Being more humble than the grass, more tolerant than the tree, giving respect to those no one respects, one should always sing the kiirtana of the Lord.")

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

The history of Ananda Marga is a chronicle of chilling events and happenings that is both frightfully thrilling and poignant. The harrowing tales of injustice and oppression are hair-raising. The stigmatization of the Marga, the slander campaign, and the gross ill-treatment of the Margiis, are painfully chronicled, and deep anguish and distress are written large on its pages. From time to time Margiis, were singled out and subjected to humiliation and scorn and were harassed and tormented in every way possible. The organization and its members were being systematically crushed, and the Marga Guru Himself was not spared. It is for future generations to assess and evaluate these harsh historical events and judge for themselves whether there was any justification for the virulent attacks and the concerted vilification of the Marga and its followers.

A question arises as to why Baba, in whom all the occult powers were vested and manifested, the supreme controller of all divine forces, allowed these events to take place. Why did He not save Himself, the Marga, and the Margiis from the terrible attacks of slander and calumny and all the distress and humiliation that followed? These are questions for which there are no straight-forward and easy answers. The Sadguru constantly subjects spiritual aspirants to severe tests. He does not desire to have a large band of opportunist and unethical followers. He seeks disciples who are capable of cent per cent dedication and self-surrender. Hence, from time to time and at every stage, He tests them and sifts them as one would separate chaff from grain.

Periods of difficulty and distress are the true test of a sadhaka's dedication and devotion towards his guru. The Master tosses His disciple into the most frightening situations and pushes him to the limits of his endurance. He unsettles the mind, which becomes disturbed and confused. The sadhaka's patience and resolution are severely tested through these periods of confusion and helplessness. The one who manages to remain steadfast and steady through the most trying situations, ultimately proves himself worthy of the Guru's grace.

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

In the role of the Sadguru, Baba performed some wondrous feats that were impossible and unimaginable at the human level. Simultaneously, at other times, Baba displayed a state of utter weakness and helplessness. Both these states of absolute power and absolute helplessness were equally astonishing and bewildering on account of their paradoxical contradictoriness. This apparent paradox is an inexplicable part of His liila, and it is His deliberately unfathomable style of functioning. For those who had joined the "Magician's party," these apparent contradictions were in due course resolved and clarified. But those who remained silent on-lookers were utterly confused and consequently became victims of their own misconceptions.

Those who were part of the Magician's entourage, His associates and followers, knew full well the wise adage: "What Ram ordains, comes to pass." They knew and understood that everything happens in accordance with His divine plan. After all it was only because Lord Ram had so planned that He underwent exile in the forest. Again, as per the Lord's plan, Lakshman became unconscious and Sita was abducted. So too, in this present age, if Baba had so planned that His own wife should oppose Him, that a charge of murder should be brought against Him, and that as a consequence He should remain for years as a helpless prisoner, then what recourse was there for us humans, powerless and insignificant as we are? If along with such display of apparent helplessness, Baba would in a trice and with utmost ease and casualness display some remarkable powers and perform miraculous acts, then surely there was no room for surprise or wonder!

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

The period of 1975 to 1977, which coincide with the duration of the Emergency in India, is highly significant in the annals of the organization. This period deserves special mention and needs to be studied closely. Those were the days of intense fear and exceeding terror. This goes further back to the very start of the decade, the early seventies, which marked the beginning of the hostile attitude of the Government towards the Marga, its founder, and the Margiis. First and foremost, false and foul charges were framed against the Marga Guru, and He was imprisoned. Well before the imposition of the Emergency, the attitude of the Government had been exceedingly arrogant and high handed towards the Marga. It is common knowledge now that the Marga was singled out for the harshest and most atrocious treatment during the Emergency. Consequently there was much panic and confusion within the Marga, and the hapless Margiis were greatly

alarmed. All the assets and properties of the Marga were unlawfully confiscated. A total ban was enforced on the organization and all dharmic and spiritual activities were declared illegal and hence prohibited. Almost all the sannyasiis and very many Margiis were systematically arrested and were thrust into jails. A handful of sannyasiis went into hiding, and life for them became traumatic indeed.

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

Those Margiis who were not taken into custody were continuously harassed. They were pressurized to give written testaments of having disassociated themselves from the Marga. By now the media had ceased to function independently. Controlled by the government, the press and the media became a convenient medium for anti-Marga propaganda. So it was that all sorts of slanderous articles appeared in the press. False and fabricated charges were made against the organization and its members. As a result of this well-orchestrated vilification campaign, the Margiis were subjected to great humiliation. It was impossible to defend themselves against this continuous barrage of verbal abuse and slander. Moreover, there was no freedom of speech and under such oppressive circumstances, it became difficult for them to lead a life of dignity and honour. Tormented by malicious aspersions and unfounded accusations, the Margiis were a distressed lot; their self-esteem was almost destroyed and social interaction was hard to maintain. Worst of all, Margiis who held good jobs were summarily dismissed from service, and it was hard for them to maintain their families. In short, every conceivable form of injustice, tyranny and torment was unleashed against them.

By then Indira Gandhi seemed invincible and everyone had to bend according to her wishes. No one dared to oppose her openly. Even those who disagreed with her policies did not speak out; rather they feigned support and outwardly acknowledged her supremacy. In this atmosphere of excessive fear and terror, all opposition was suppressed – the thousand-armed goddess had succeeded in effectively crushing all dissent. Indiraji's autocratic style of functioning became synonymous with her government's misrule and abuse of power.

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

It seemed as though the deities of wealth, learning and power had all come under her sway, and Indiraji appeared to radiate supreme confidence and vitality. She moved in a blaze of glory, and even Lord Jagganath's chariot could not vie with the pomp and patronage that surrounded Indiraji's cavalcade. Portraits of the Lady looked down from every square and cross-road. During the entire period of the Emergency, Indiraji ruled supreme. Her agents and sycophants were beyond the law and could get away with anything. The maxim "Vaedikii himsá, himsn'na bhavati" ("killing [animals] in Vedic rituals is not violence") was conveniently acknowledged and applied. So it was that Indiraji's rule epitomized the pinnacle of unbridled tyranny. No one dared to criticize her policies or express a contrary opinion. The ordinary citizen now turned a blind eye to all the atrocities that were being committed. Those who did venture to question or oppose these had to face dire consequences. The state machinery had successfully unleashed a reign of terror, and the voice of the common man was effectively silenced. As a result of the fear psychosis, people began to regard her with awe and many shuddered at the mere mention of her name. Indiraji's reputation grew by leaps and bounds, and the authority she exercised and the powers she wielded gave her the status of a demi-god!

Under these trying circumstances, many Margiis were shaken and their faith in their Guru began to waver. To them Baba's enormous and awesome powers appeared to be of no match to Indiraji's all-encompassing power and capabilities. The balance was tilted in Madam's favour, and undoubtedly she appeared invincible to many. In fact, the initial reaction of fear gradually turned into awe and admiration for this all-powerful lady.

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

This was the state of affairs in the year 1975. No one in his wildest dreams could have anticipated or imagined this chain of events in 1971. That was the year when the Margiis were drenched in the blissful waves of devotion. The years 1969 to 1971 were the "demonstration years." During that period Margiis had personally witnessed Baba's many "demonstrations" of various siddhis, occult powers. Many fortunate Margiis had experienced various stages of samadhi as Baba performed these "experiments" on their body and mind. It was a blissful period of great spiritual upliftment, which seemed to have dispelled the illusory veil of Maya, much as sadhana dispels the clouds of mental unease. Those present during these demonstrations experienced a state of indescribable awe and wonder, as though they had been seated in the Lord's own royal court. They were so fortunate indeed, that without any effort on their part, they had been graced to experience and witness these high spiritual states. All they could say afterwards was that they were oblivious of everything around them, transported as they were into the seventh heaven! It was an awe-

inspiring, wondrous sight, and a unique congregation for whom the flood gates of devotion breached all mental barriers.

In those days, saturated as they were with bhakti, devotion, there was no room for doubt or disbelief. Set in those very days is a story that throws light on Baba's capacity to see into the future, His divine vision. This wondrous tale is being related here for the benefit of the reader.

Shri Brajabihariji of Patna is a very senior Margii and is one of Baba's closest devotees. In July 1971 Baba was admitted for treatment at the Patna Medical College Hospital. During Baba's stay in the hospital, Brajabihariji was blessed with the wonderful opportunity to serve Him. He was indeed very fortunate. This story is related to those very days when Baba was in hospital.

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

One day, Baba quite unexpectedly and without any apparent reason said to Brajabihariji: "Tell me, Brajabihari, if in the near future, circumstances were to become grave and adverse, you won't be afraid will you?"

"No Baba!" Brajabihariji replied boldly.

"Even in the most adverse circumstances you will not be afraid?"

This time there was a note of alarm in Baba's question. "No Baba!" came the reply, a confident assertion of his faith.

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

"Are you sure you will not be afraid in the most trying and terrifying situation?" Baba was testing him again. Secure in his Guru's shelter, Brajabihariji replied, "When You are there, why should I be afraid?"

Baba became grave and in a voice that seemed to suggest terror and fear, He said, "You know Brajabihari, in 1975, circumstances will be such that the majority of the Margiis will be leaving the Marga, even as rats abandon a sinking ship. Won't you be afraid then?"

Once more Brajabihariji replied confidently: "No Baba! Never will I be afraid. I am not like the rat. I am more like a bird. There is only one refuge for a bird. It may fly off the ship only to return to its safety. So also for us devotees, You are our only refuge, where else can we go?"

To which Baba replied: "Listen Brajabihari! There will come a day when even a close and dear Margii brother will refuse to acknowledge your greeting. He will do his utmost to shun you and put you off. Indeed, if he sees you approaching he will change his path in order to avoid you. He will not look you in the face and if you force him to talk to you, he will feign surprise and ask as to why you want to talk to him, since he is not a Margii." Brajabihariji was now in a quandary. He was perplexed by Baba's strange conversation.

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

Changing the topic he tried to coax Baba to take some rest. He said to Him: "Baba! You are very tired today, please try to sleep for a while."

But Baba refused to change the topic and continued to speak: "It is possible that a time may come when the people will lose their faith in me. No sign of Ananda Marga will be found for miles on end! Brajabihari, in all probability such a situation will arise." Baba very clearly told this to Brajabihariji way back in 1971, at a time when there was no sign or hint of such situation. No one in his normal senses could have anticipated such a turn of events in the near future. But if the Lord has drawn up His plan, then in a trice the impossible takes place.

The sweetly intoxicating golden period of 1970-1971 was followed by a devastating storm which left everyone shocked and shaken. Baba was arrested and imprisoned in December 1971 and He spent seven years in jail. During that period He remained without food for nearly five years. He had to face exceeding hostility and was subjected to unbearable torments, tactics used to demoralize Him and break His spirit. But Baba remained firm and adamant. Pressure was brought on Him and Baba was clearly and unambiguously asked to dissolve the organization. For this they offered Him many baits, many temptations to entice Him into meeting their demands. The government officials dealing with Baba were extremely arrogant and uncouth, and their behaviour was harsh and intimidating. They were bent on crushing His spirit, and though they tried every vile and cunning means to further their evil intent, they could not succeed. Frustrated and thwarted in their efforts, they finally administered poison in the guise of medication. So it was that history repeated itself and every foul means was employed to silence effectively the voice of dharma.

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

The year 1974 was drawing to an end. In those days, Baba's PA and the General Secretary of the Marga, Acarya Keshavanandaji, would take turns in visiting Him in jail. On one of those occasions when Keshavanandaji happened to visit Him, Baba said to him, "Keshavananda! Go out and inform all the Indian Margiis that they should immediately make provisions to store two years' ration in their homes. If all else cannot be managed, they should at least store rice, dal and salt." Baba further clarified that this was mandatory only for the householder Margiis, and not for the wholetime sannyasii workers. When the Margiis learned that they had to compulsorily follow this order, they were totally stunned. The possibility of a severe drought

and famine left them anxious and puzzled. At the same time they considered themselves fortunate and specially graced, for they had been pre-warned and alerted about the on-coming calamity. However they could not comprehend as to why this "order" was meant only for the householders. Why was it not applicable for the sannyasiis as well? No one had an adequate answer to this question.

Finally, on 26th June, 1975, a state of Emergency was declared. That day will forever be recorded as a black page in the annals of Indian history. The problem of one individual's safety and security came to be synonymous with an entire nation's so-called security. Just a couple of days before the imposition of the Emergency, Indiraji herself must not have been aware of the drastic steps she would need to take in order to perpetuate her rule. Nor could she have anticipated the calamity that was about to befall the Marga. But Baba had already given a clear indication of these events four years in advance. In retrospect it is now evident that the entire cycle of events was a play of His own divine liila.

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

At the time of the imposition of the Emergency, perhaps Indiraji herself would not have thought that its duration would be so long. As time went by, Indiraji came to enjoy absolute power and unrestricted authority. Infatuated with the thrill of power and position Indiraji appeared unassailable, and it seemed that the Emergency would continue for an indefinite period; in fact it could last for ever! There was no freedom of speech and constitutional rights were effectively clamped. All this was declared to be done in the interests of the nation. Even as Indiraji's autocratic rule tightened its vice-like grip over the entire nation, hopes of democratic rights ever being restored receded, fading like a dream.

There was a sense of despair and utter helplessness in the ranks of the political opponents, as they all languished in jail. Thus it was that eighteen months rolled by.

As for the Margiis who had been imprisoned, all of them experienced a special grace and blessing from Baba. It was due to this special grace that throughout the long and seemingly endless period of the Emergency, they remained optimistic and spirited. All through the period of their imprisonment, their morale remained amazingly high. Hamrahi, insignificant as he is, would like to state that this was also his very own personal experience. Baba had sent a wonderful and inspiring message to all the imprisoned Margiis. He advised them to remain firm in their sadhana and strictly follow the Sixteen Points. He assured the Margiis that these seemingly irrepressible forces would be destroyed by their own accord.

All the politicians who were behind bars for the long and indefinite period of the Emergency were dispirited and depressed. However, they could not help marveling at their Ananda Margii fellow prisoners, who enthusiastically and cheerfully sang bhajanas and kiirtanaa and were regular in their spiritual practices. Nothing seemed to dampen their courage and zeal and for this they were much admired by the other political prisoners. Themselves afraid and demoralized, some of them began to question the Ananda Marga sannyasiis. They asked them: "Since you all do so much sadhana and puja, surely you must be knowing as to how long this Emergency will last?"

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

To this the Dadas would reply: "Oh no! We do not practice sadhana with the purpose of gaining spiritual powers to know the past or the future; that is never our aim. We know as much about the future as you all do. However, we differ in our attitudes, we are supremely optimistic about the future; there is no room for pessimism."

On being probed further the Dadas would reply, "You know, the situation appears the same to us as well. As per the present circumstances it is not likely that the Emergency will be lifted in the near future. However, prior to the imposition of the Emergency, our Gurudeva, through a special announcement, had specifically alerted and cautioned all the Margiis. They had been warned to store up sufficient basic rations to last them for two years. From this perhaps we can surmise that the Emergency may not last beyond a period of two years."

Inspite of this seemingly hopeless situation, the sannyasiis maintained full faith in their Guru, and with such words of encouragement, they continued to boost the morale of their fellow political prisoners.

There is an oft repeated saying which goes, "divine retribution may be slow in coming, but it is always just and fair." The wisdom and verity of this popular adage was clearly realized when the seemingly endless period of the Emergency was brought to an end within a span of just two years. There was a sense of disbelief amongst the general public when nineteen months after the declaration of the Emergency, Indiraji called for a general election. Even this announcement failed to stir up the spirits of the demoralized politicians. They were suspicious of Indiraji's motives. When the

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

majority of the political leaders were languishing in jail, the election was bound to be a one-sided affair. They were sure that Indiraji would win un-opposed and the election would turn out to be a farce.

The majority of the national leaders initially decided to boycott the elections. However, good sense prevailed and they all decided to contest the elections under a united front. The elections released the pent up spirits of an oppressed nation and Indira Gandhi suffered the most crushing defeat in her political career. It was a bitter pill to swallow. Within two years, rather twenty-one months to be exact, the "Indira Chapter" was finally closed. Moreover, the "black page" in the annals of Indian history was turned over even as the Emergency was finally lifted. Simultaneous with the lifting of the Emergency, the ban on the Marga was also lifted.

It was entirely due to Baba's grace that the Marga and the Margiis could ultimately withstand the tyrannical pressures of the Emergency. While all the activities of the Marga were banned in India, there was during that very period an unprecedented growth of the organization overseas. Around about 1971, the Marga had its presence in about five countries; but by 1978, however, the Marga had established itself in eighty countries. In India, Margiis who had held government posts were heavily compensated and received their due promotions. In short, the overall benefits reaped by the Marga far outweighed the grave hardships and loss it had suffered during the Emergency. The suffering and humiliation that was experienced was indeed a part and parcel of His liila, and so also the relief that followed was due to the incomparable grace of the most merciful and compassionate Baba. Whatever was to happen, did so as per His wish. Ultimately, all that took place happened in accordance with His pre-determined resolve. In a subtle and mysterious way, all events were controlled by Him; nothing could take place without His will.

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

In this context, a tale worth relating comes to mind. It is about Shri Ramchanderji, a resident of Coimbatore in the State of Tamilnadu. As mentioned earlier, during the Emergency, the householder Margiis were relentlessly pursued by the police and they were under constant surveillance. However, Ramchanderji narrowly escaped from this traumatic experience. For this miraculous escape, there is only one answer. That is: "What the Lord ordained, came to pass."

At the time of his initiation, Ramchanderji was already a middle-aged man. He was very regular and strict in his sadhana practice. Soon after his initiation, Ramchanderji was fortunate to get personal contact with Baba at one of the Dharma Mahacakras. Overcome with emotion he said to Baba: "Baba! I would like to be an active worker of the Marga. Please give me some special responsibility."

Baba replied that owing to his age, he would not be able to do any active work. However, Baba assured him that he could play the role of an active worker by giving financial support to the Marga. Right away Ramchanderji agreed to do so. Ramchanderji was a businessman, and invariably there exists a mutual hostility between the police and the business community. On that very occasion of his personal contact, Ramchanderji expressed his fears and fervently requested Baba to grant him full protection from the police. Indeed he sought a special blessing from Baba that the police may never come near him. Baba must have been in a very good mood that day, for He forthwith replied: "Tathastu" ("let it be thus"), and blessed him with the varabhaya mudra.

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

From that very first meeting with Baba, Ramchanderji began to give steady and substantial financial assistance to the Marga. Every month without fail, he would send to the Center a money order of one thousand rupees. In those days a monthly contribution of one thousand rupees meant a lot for the organization. Apart from that, Ramchanderji would give substantial help to any sannyasii worker who would come to his house. No matter how many workers approached him, he would help them all in proportion to the urgency of their need and the scope of the work that needed to be done. Each time he would help someone, he would ideate on Baba and like the humble poet he would say to them that he was only following Baba's dictate, and so they should accept it as His grace alone. Quoting the lines of the poet he would say that he was not obliging them in any way:

Dendar kou aur hai, det rahat din reyn
Log bharam ham pe dhare, ya so niche nain.

("The Giver is another indeed, day and night He gifts. Lest they may suspect me, my eyes I dare not lift.")

The Emergency was imposed; all the prominent Margii brothers became victims of police aggression and were hauled in. But Ramchanderji was not harmed in any way. The police in all its force had been assigned to arrest the Margiis, householders and sannyasiis, and all the state powers were fully utilized for this purpose. In spite of this, no one ever cast as much as a glance at Ramchanderji. Even though the situation had become alarming, Ramchanderji did not go into

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

hiding, nor did he, like so many fearful Margiis, severe his ties with the Marga.

During the Emergency, many sannyasii Dadas had to go into hiding and moved around in civilian clothes. Whenever these Dadas came to Ramchanderji's house, he would unhesitatingly give them shelter and help them in every way. The Dadas lived under the constant threat of possible arrest. So for their own safety, they would not remain in one place for more than a day or two. Reassuring these 'refugees', Ramchanderji would often say: "Dada, you can stay in my house without any hesitation for as long as you wish. You need not worry about your safety here, for the police will not step into this house." Thumping his chest, he would confidently say that his house had become a "safe haven" by the grace of Baba's varabhaya mudra. "The police dare not look towards my house, and if they do so they will surely lose their sight!"

He used to relate that right from 1971, when Baba had been taken into custody, the secret police had begun to harass almost every Margii in Coimbatore. They would go to their houses and interrogate them, posing all sorts of irrelevant questions just to intimidate the Margiis. But no one ever came to Ramchanderji's house; no one dared peep into his house. Nor will anyone dare to do so in the future, for the simple reason that Baba had blessed him with the special blessing during his personal contact. Ramchanderji would boldly relate all this with full faith and assurance. But the Dadas would not accept his assurances and would say, "You have received the varabhaya mudra, not us. We cannot take the risk."

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

As a matter of fact, all through the period of the Emergency and after, not even the shadow of the police fell upon Ramchanderji's house. The strangest part of it was that during the Emergency, the Coimbatore police would often question various other Margiis about the identity and whereabouts of Ramchanderji. But amazing as it seems, the police never succeeded in securing the address of one of the most prominent Margiis in Coimbatore. It is all the more astonishing considering the fact that the police had seized all records and documents, including scraps and waste, from the Central Office of the Marga. The office was then sealed. Every paper was minutely scrutinized. In fact, all the mail addressed to the Central Office was being censored well before the imposition of the Emergency. In spite of such careful scrutiny, it is incredible that the police could not get hold of the address of Ramchanderji, who was sending a regular monthly money order to the Central Office!

Throughout the length and breadth of the country, none of the Margiis who were financially supporting the Marga were spared. Only Ramchanderji was left alone, and he lived through those traumatic times moving about freely without any fear of arrest. In those dreaded days, anyone having even a remote connection with the Marga was harassed. So it was that even the non-Margii parents and guardians of children attending Ananda Marga schools were not spared. They were repeatedly interrogated and subjected to humiliating investigations. Under such trying circumstances, only Ramchanderji had come through the Emergency totally unscathed. There is only one explanation for this, that it was undoubtedly a great miracle!

It would be unjust to conclude that those Margiis who had to undergo imprisonment did not enjoy Baba's grace in equal measure. Those who were destined to suffer, suffered. Those who were destined to be spared, were spared. All this was a part of His liila, and the events could only reflect His pre-determined plan. Ultimately, what the Lord had ordained, took place.

Indiraji herself was a mere pawn in His divine game of chess.

"What the Lord Ordains Comes to Pass"

---

Náham manye suvedeti no na vedeti veda ca,
Yo nas tad veda tad veda no na vedeti veda ca.
("I do not think “It is well known,” nor do I think “It is not known”; one of us who understands, understands It, and one who thinks he understands, does not.")

Emboldened Sanjaya's Heroic Defiance

Dharmakshetre kurukshetre samavetâ yuyutsavah,
Mámakáh pándavásh caivn kim akurvata Sanjaya?

("On the field of Dharma, Kurukshetra, where my party and the Pandavas have assembled intent on battle, what took place, O Sanjaya?")

Sanjaya's role in the Mahabharata was largely played behind the scenes, in the background. However, his is a significant part, one of considerable importance. Mahasambhuti Lord Krishna's universally revered and sacred treatise "The Bhagavadgiita" opens with the aforementioned shloka; and Sanjaya's pivotal role is acknowledged in that very first opening stanza. The jiiva, blinded and ensnared in the delusory veil of maya, is symbolized in the person of the blind King Dhrtarashthra. It was impossible for him to comprehend the real significance of the Krishna Liila unfolding on the battlefield of Kurukshetra. Visually handicapped as he was, he remained totally absorbed in the events on the battlefield, and was ever anxious to know the swiftly unfolding drama of victory and defeat, of gain or reversal of fortune. True realization and clear understanding follows in the light of one's spiritual insight. Sanjaya was the embodiment of wise discrimination, the voice of conscience and discernment, through the cultivation of which one gains spiritual insight.

Emboldened Sanjaya's Heroic Defiance

Amongst the many disciples of Shrii Shrii Anandamurtiji, the Mahasambhuti of the present era, there was indeed such a Sanjaya, one equally blessed with the gift of spiritual insight. During His lifetime, Baba rarely displayed His divine and spiritual powers; such an explicit demonstration was reserved for a few rare occasions. Rather, His divinity was revealed far more subtly, and invariably through indirect means. On one such occasion, Hamrahi too had the good fortune of becoming one of His insignificant and worthless agents. This goes back to the explicit and authoritative demonstration days of the "Sadhana Year." That was the time when Hamrahi, trapped in the formidable net of samskaras, had become a victim of inferiority complexes. Baba never tolerated such mental complexes and so He blessed Hamrahi with a golden opportunity, which finally delivered him from his predicament. In the midst of a full assembly of disciples, Baba called upon Hamrahi. Thrusting His stick – that wondrous dispeller of all troubles – into his hands, Baba ordered him to touch anyone's ajna cakra and assured him that whosoever he thus touched, would experience samadhi. And so it was, whomsoever Hamrahi touched was immediately transported into a state of samadhi!

During His Jamalpur days, Dasharathji was Baba's most prominent and significant "agent." He was one of the most spiritually elevated, responsible and celebrated members of the Marga. Due to his spiritual insight, people came to look upon him as another Sanjaya. From time to time Baba would bless him with the special grace of omniscience. Lightly built, Dasharathji was a picture of frail sensitivity. His simple life-style coupled with a high level of sadhana practices was a source of inspiration to all who knew him. His was a considerably strong and powerful personality encased albeit in a frail and feeble body. Physically, he suffered from a slight visual handicap, but as far as his spiritual vision was concerned, there was none to measure up to his accurate insight.

Emboldened Sanjaya's Heroic Defiance

In those Jamalpur days, whenever Baba wanted to show the sadhakas their previous or earlier lives, He would place His hands behind Dasharathji's head, or at times He would just transmit His spiritual power directly without actually touching Dasharathji. Thus empowered, Dasharathji would be able to look into the previous life of any sadhaka; not just one life, he would accurately reveal glimpses of several lives in the sadhaka's past. In the case of Hamrahi, Dasharathji was able to look into his immediate past life, lived in Sri Lanka. He then went on to reveal details of one of his previous births, spanning many life-times, in an African colony. Whenever Dasharath Dada would be present during one of Baba's darshanas, it would be taken for granted that someone's previous life experiences would be revealed that day. Under normal circumstances, Dasharathji lived the life of any other ordinary person. But in the presence of Baba, by His mere will, Dasharathji would be instantly transported into a state of omniscience. Indeed he would be able to transcend all limitations of time, space and person. He would become transcendental!

Dasharathji was a school teacher in Jamalpur and later he became the Headmaster too. On the recommendation of Acarya Shishir Dutta, he was initiated in 1955. The incident regarding his initiation has been related elsewhere in this book, under the chapter entitled "The

Desire for Liberation brings the Sadguru." Dasharathji had received special lessons from Baba of "Vishesha Yoga," and after the Lord's Mahaprayan, the highest honour of "Purodha" was bestowed upon him. Undoubtedly, he enjoyed a very senior and much respected status in the Marga. He was the Chief Examiner for all the trainees studying for the post of Acaryaship. In the transition between the two millenniums, on the first of January 2000, this divine soul left this world to be one with the Supreme Consciousness. The liila-story that follows is related to this very same person.

Emboldened Sanjaya's Heroic Defiance

This incident is about the time when the Marga had just been formally established. Its very first Jagrti, situated in Rampur Colony, was very small. In 1958, Baba directed the Margiis to establish another more spacious Jagrti in Jamalpur. The Jagrti, as a symbol of spiritual renaissance, was a unique product of Baba's imagination and foresight. Its very first foundation was laid in Jamalpur, and in the course of time, many such Jagrtis were established all over the world.

For the handful of sadhakas in Jamalpur, this was their first major project, highly significant but excessively difficult to conceive and execute. Under normal circumstances it appeared to be an immense task. With much effort and careful inquiry, a plot of land, situated in Aulipur Mohalla, was purchased. This is the very same sacred spot on which the present Jagrti stands, this very sanctified Jagrti, with which are associated innumerable sacred and blissful memories of Baba's many liilas.

It so happened that the purchase of the land was frowned upon by certain anti-social elements, who did not approve of this. Baba always maintained that struggle is the essence of life, both in the individual and collective spheres. For one who strongly advocated and instilled the spirit of struggle in His disciples, it was but expected that there would be great obstacles and difficulties in materializing His first important "wish." Whatever the organization has achieved has been done through great struggle, and through much blood-shed and toil—which is why its victories are sweet and enduring.

Emboldened Sanjaya's Heroic Defiance

There was a notorious criminal who lived in Jamalpur who went by the name of "Bachhu." Bachhu's gang too had their eyes on this very plot of land. When they learnt of the land being purchased by the Marga, they were enraged. The issue became a matter of prestige for them, and they were determined to seize the land and gain unlawful possession of the same. A dispute followed, and they resorted to violence. By the use of force and arms they managed to occupy the land. The majority of the Marga sadhakas belonged to the class of intelligentsia, white-collar workers. They were not the sort to participate in any acts of violence or rioting and shuddered at the thought of having to confront the goons. Some of the sadhakas plucked up courage to approach Bachhu with the request that they should be allowed to re-establish their legal right over the land. But they were intimidated by the strong-arm tactics of the gang, and were forced to retreat. Frustrated and humiliated they returned to Rampur Colony and reported the matter to Baba.

Baba was upset, enraged by their lack of courage and seemingly disgraceful and 'effeminate' behaviour. He burst out: "Put on bangles and eyeliner and hide in your homes! You all are incapable of accomplishing this task. I will get this work done through my daughters, who are certainly more courageous than all of you!" Baba's scathing words hurt their sense of manliness and valour. The Margiis were very upset and disturbed. But they felt helpless as no one could muster up the pluck and courage to directly confront Bachhu and his gang.

Emboldened Sanjaya's Heroic Defiance

All of a sudden Dasharathji felt overwhelmed by a great impulse of emotion, as though an unexpected transmission of courage and boldness had taken hold of his being. Charged with this irrepressible and overflowing emotion, Dasharathji determined to physically confront Bachhu and promptly set off towards the Jagrti plot. All those who witnessed this great surge of courage and pluck in Dasharathji's otherwise frail body were astounded. As Dasharathji approached Bachhu with a firm resolve to 'battle-it-out' once and for all, the latter was enraged. He pulled out his "bhujali" and charged towards Dasharathji. His intentions were wicked indeed. It was apparent that he wanted to do away with Dasharathji and thus terrorise the Margiis; this way he hoped to settle the matter in his favour. By getting rid of Dasharathji, Bachhu hoped to instill dread and fear in the minds of the Margiis.

A "bhujali" is a shorter version of a sword, a small spear-like weapon, with a murderously sharp edge. Just a blow is enough to kill a person instantaneously. Dasharath Dada was an exceedingly simple and straight-forward person. Without any fear or care for the impending death that hung over him, he sought to confront Bachhu with only his umbrella in hand! From the start it was an uneven contest, the sword against the needle, the wicked, cruel and intimidating Bachhu against the frail and feeble Dasharathda. Those watching from afar stood speechless and paralyzed with fear, expecting the worst to befall the

frail schoolmaster! No one had the courage to come forward and try and save him.

At this very moment something unbelievable took place. As Bachhu approached, "bhujali" in hand to strike a fatal blow upon Dasharathji, his hand suddenly became motionless. Try as he did he could not strike, the hand remained motionless in that very position. Grasping the opportunity, the on-lookers rushed in and rescued Dasharathji from what would have been a certain calamity. Dasharathji immediately went to see Baba. This time there was a tremendous change in Baba's look and expression. No doubt nothing had changed from the last meeting with Baba, and the matter still remained unresolved. But instead of the previous outburst of anger and rage, Baba now greeted them with great joy.

Emboldened Sanjaya's Heroic Defiance

He said, "Well done! Well done! This is the sort of courage I always want to see in my sons. One should never compromise with immoral principles. Do not withdraw when faced with difficulties, be prepared to struggle. It is not a question of victory or defeat. It is a matter of moral courage, of strength of mind and spirit. That is the strength that Dasharath displayed today. He fought in the face of death itself. As I sat here, I had to intervene by means of "Stambhana-kriya" and halted the attack. After all, Dasharath's righteous effort should not have ended up as an impulsive act of rashness."

With their trust and faith in Baba's divine intervention and reassured by His words of encouragement, the Margiis gathered all the sadhakas from the neighbouring villages, districts and towns. With their collective strength they were finally able to retrieve their land and hoisted their flag so as to establish their legal right over their property. Check-mated, Bachhu was now a dispirited man. Never again did that dreaded terrorist dare to confront the Marga.

This then is the true import and meaning of "Ahimsa," which Baba clearly emphasized and clarified for all times to come. To put up with injustice in the name of ahimsa is the first sign of cowardice. It is this very cowardice that is exploited by the tyrants in order to perpetuate their tyranny over the oppressed. Those who only acknowledge the language of violence and blows should be dealt with in like manner. In reality every tyrant is at heart a veritable coward. He cannot endure and face up to any righteous confrontation which is backed by true courage and valour. When faced with a vigorous and stout resistance, these immoral elements are invariably defeated, for there is no solid foundation, no moral strength to support their so-called success. It is possible that in the course of clash and conflict, the righteous too may have to suffer loss. But that is to be expected; for has it not been said, "After all, only the valiant riders fall in the field of battle!"

Emboldened Sanjaya's Heroic Defiance

Atiitah panthánam, tava ca mahimá vánmanasayor,
atad vyávrttyá yam cakitam abhidhatte shrutir api;
sa kasya stotavyah katividhagunah kasya vishayah,
pade tv arváciine patati na manah kasya na vacah.
("Your greatness goes beyond the paths of speech and mind. Who can praise You, whom even the Veda hesitantly describes with the evasive "You are not that"? For whom can You, who possesses so many kinds of virtue, be an object of knowledge? But whose mind and speech do not attempt this?")

"Tell me, who is mad, you or I?"

The divine presence radiates infinite joy that is truly transcendental. Even a fleeting experience of this divine bliss can at times trigger a state of abnormality, for the devotee's heart and mind are incapable of containing this great joy. The few who have experienced this momentary state of rapture tend to lose control of themselves. This brings about a transformation in their behaviour pattern. The ordinary person caught up in his worldly affairs cannot comprehend such a lofty state, and in his ignorance he dismisses it as a case of insanity, eccentricity or sheer madness. Due to his ignorance and foolishness, the worldly person is unable to empathize with the pain of the devotee who suffers from the pangs of separation from his beloved. No wonder Mira Bai, lost in her deep love and longing for her beloved Lord cried out, "No one can understand my pain." Neither can the world fathom their pain, nor are they able to explain it to the world at large. In an attempt to explain this in the tradition of the Sufi saints, a poet says:

Verily this madness of ours is a blessing in disguise
For how would one explain all to the worldly 'wise'!

Seized and transported by the rapture of divine love, the devotee cannot help but lose himself. In that trance-like state of mental disturbance, he appears abnormal to the outside world, which is only too ready to pronounce him as mad or crazed. This moving story is about one such individual whom society dismissed as insane.

"Tell me, who is mad, you or I?"

Although Baba used to be surrounded by Margiis, who constantly hovered around Him, many amongst them had not yet fully realized His divine nature. On the other hand there were some highly evolved non-Margii sadhakas who had fathomed His true nature. This story goes back to around the year 1945 when Ananda Marga had not yet been founded. Baba used to reside in Keshavpur Mohalla in Jamalpur and the pattern of His life was no different from that of any other ordinary citizen. In those days, Keshavpur Mohalla was still a quiet locality. Very often in the evenings, Baba would draw up a chair and sit outside His house and read the daily newspaper. Right opposite His house was a large bungalow belonging to an aristocratic Bengali family. In front of the house was a large verandah with an open platform adjoining it. Ever so often an apparently deranged person would come and seat himself on this platform. From his dress, his bearing and general demeanour, it was obvious that the man was out of his senses. Generally he would sit there silent and quiet, and not a word would come out of him. People used to provoke him by calling him "mad" or even "mad and dumb," and children would make fun of him. Sometimes they could be cruel and would pelt him with stones. Inspite of the provocation he remained indifferent and never ever reacted. Indeed he bore all this silently and betrayed no emotion. In fact he appeared drained of all passion, speech and life itself!

"Tell me, who is mad, you or I?"

An adolescent teenager by the name of Rameshwar Baidha also lived in that same locality. One day Rameshwar and his friend Ganesh happened to pass by on their way home from school. Some of the boys from the area were playing cards on that same platform on which "mad and dumb" was seated. For the ordinary person, bound by his karmic inclinations, there is a strange attraction for that which is palpably wrong. So also, Rameshwar and Ganesh were drawn towards the boys and they stood by watching their game. Bad company is infectious and hard to resist. Very soon Ganesh expressed his desire to play a hand in the card game. Rameshwar had a dislike for cards and he wanted to move on. But his friend Ganesh assured him that he would only play one game and so persuaded Rameshwar to wait for him. As a loyal friend, Rameshwar agreed to wait. But Ganesh did not get up after the game was over, in fact he continued to play for several hands after that. Impatient and annoyed, Rameshwar burst out, "If you do not stop now, I am definitely leaving."

Taking undue advantage of his friend's loyalty, Ganesh pleaded, "That's it now. This is my last game, no more after that."

Ganesh had barely spoken these words when the usually quiet and emotionless lunatic burst out laughing. His laughter was loud and uninhibited. After a while he collected himself and in a loud and solemn voice declared: "Look here! How foolish are these residents of Jamalpur! They are absolutely stupid. The Supreme Being has taken birth in their very town. He has been working in the Jamalpur Railway Workshop and these ignorant fools are making no effort to come to know Him. Instead they are whiling away their most precious
